\displaystyle\hat{\sigma}_{\mathrm{Int},1}^{qq\to q,5}(v,w,\zeta)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{N_{c}}\frac{w}{4(1-\zeta)\zeta(1-v)^{4}}\left[v(w-\zeta)^{2}\left(\zeta+v^{2}\left(\zeta+(4\zeta-2)w^{2}-2\zeta w\right)\right.\right.

(177)

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\left.+v\left(\left(8\zeta^{2}-6\zeta-2\right)w-2\zeta^{2}\right)\right)+\text{sgn}(w-\zeta)\left(v^{3}(w-\zeta)^{2}\left(\zeta+2w^{2}-2\zeta w\right)\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt-2v^{2}\left(\zeta^{4}+(\zeta-1)w^{3}+\zeta^{2}w^{2}+\left(\zeta-3\zeta^{3}\right)w\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\left.\left.+\zeta v\left(\zeta^{2}+(5-4\zeta)w^{2}+2\zeta(2\zeta-3)w\right)+2(\zeta-1)\zeta w\right)\right]\,,

\displaystyle\hat{\sigma}_{\mathrm{Int},2}^{qq\to q,1}(v,w,\zeta)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N_{c}}\frac{v\,w}{4\,\zeta\,(1-v)^{4}}\Bigg(\frac{2\zeta^{2}-3\zeta-2v^{2}(1-w-\zeta)^{2}\text{sgn}(1-w-\zeta)}{1-\zeta}

(178)

\displaystyle\hskip-85.35826pt+\frac{v^{2}\left(2\zeta^{2}-3\zeta+2\left(4\zeta^{2}-6\zeta+1\right)w^{2}-2\left(4\zeta^{2}-5\zeta+1\right)w+1\right)+2(\zeta-1)v(w-\zeta)+1}{1-\zeta}

\displaystyle\hskip-85.35826pt+\frac{1-v}{\left[1-2v(1-w-\zeta+2\zeta w)+v^{2}(1-w-\zeta)^{2}\right]^{\tfrac{3}{2}}}\Big(2\zeta+v^{4}(1-2w)(1-w-\zeta)^{3}

\displaystyle\hskip-56.9055pt+v^{3}\left((1-\zeta)^{2}\left(2\zeta^{2}-3\zeta-2\right)+(5-6\zeta)w^{3}+\left(-6\zeta^{2}+17\zeta-12\right)w^{2}\right.

\displaystyle\hskip-56.9055pt\left.+\left(2\zeta^{3}+\zeta^{2}-12\zeta+9\right)w\right)+v^{2}\left(3\zeta\left(2\zeta^{2}-5\zeta+3\right)+\left(3-4\zeta^{2}\right)w^{2}\right.

\displaystyle\hskip-56.9055pt\left.-\left(4\zeta^{3}-22\zeta^{2}+12\zeta+3\right)w\right)-v\left(-6\zeta^{2}+9\zeta+12\zeta^{2}w-12\zeta w+w-2\right)-1\Big)\Bigg)\,,

\displaystyle\hat{\sigma}_{\mathrm{Int},2}^{qq\to q,5}(v,w,\zeta)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N_{c}}\frac{v\,w}{4\,\zeta\,(1-v)^{4}}\Bigg(\frac{-\zeta-2v^{2}(1-\zeta-w)^{2}\,\text{sgn}(1-w-\zeta)}{1-\zeta}

(179)

\displaystyle\hskip-85.35826pt+\frac{v^{2}\left(-\zeta+(2-4\zeta)w^{2}-2(1-\zeta)w+1\right)+2(1-\zeta)v((4\zeta-1)w-\zeta)+1}{1-\zeta}

\displaystyle\hskip-85.35826pt+\frac{1-v}{\left[1-2v(1-w-\zeta+2\zeta w)+v^{2}(1-w-\zeta)^{2}\right]^{\tfrac{3}{2}}}\Big(v^{4}(1-w-\zeta)^{3}(1-2w-2\zeta)

\displaystyle\hskip-56.9055pt+v^{3}\big[(1-\zeta)^{2}(5\zeta-2)+(5-16\zeta)w^{3}+\left(-32\zeta^{2}+45\zeta-12\right)w^{2}

D.2.3
Soft-Fermion Pole Contribution

At last, our formula Eq. (
71
) for the soft-fermion pole contribution concludes our results for the
qq\to q
-channel and the partonic factors therein are given as,

\displaystyle\hat{\sigma}_{\mathrm{SFP},F}^{qq\to q,1}(v,w,\chi_{m})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{-1}{2N_{c}}\Bigg[\frac{2v^{3}w\left(2w^{2}-2w+1\right)+v^{2}\left(-6w^{2}+2w-1\right)+4vw-1}{(1-v)^{4}}\,\ln\chi_{m}

(184)

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt+\frac{vw(1+v(1-4w))}{(1-v)^{3}}\,\ln(1-v)+\frac{2v^{2}w^{2}}{(1-v)^{3}}\,\ln(1-v\,w)-\frac{vw(1+v-2vw)}{(1-v)^{3}}\,\ln(1-v+v\,w)

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt+\Bigg(\frac{v}{(1-v)^{2}\,w}-\frac{1+4v}{(1-v)^{2}}+\frac{v\left(7v^{2}-8v+5\right)w}{(1-v)^{4}}-\frac{2v^{2}(3v+1)w^{2}}{(1-v)^{4}}+\frac{4v^{3}w^{3}}{(1-v)^{4}}\Bigg)\,\ln(1-w)

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt+\frac{2v^{3}(5-2w)w^{2}+v^{2}\left(-2w^{2}+6w-1\right)+2vw-1}{(1-v)^{4}}\ln w-\frac{8v^{3}w^{3}}{(1-v)^{4}}+\frac{\left(7v^{3}-8v+4\right)w}{(1-v)^{4}}

\displaystyle-\frac{v^{3}+v^{2}-7v+3}{(1-v)^{4}}+\frac{2v(3v-1)w^{2}}{(1-v)^{4}}+\frac{1}{(1-v)\,(1-v+v\,w)}\Bigg]\,,

\displaystyle\hat{\sigma}_{\mathrm{SFP},G}^{qq\to q,5}(v,w,\chi_{m})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{-1}{2N_{c}}\Bigg[\frac{2v^{3}w\left(2w^{2}-2w+1\right)+v^{2}\left(-6w^{2}+2w-1\right)+4vw-1}{(1-v)^{4}}\,\,\ln\chi_{m}

(185)

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt+\frac{v\,(1+v)\,w}{(1-v)^{3}}\,\ln(1-v)-\frac{2v^{2}w^{2}}{(1-v)^{3}}\,\ln(1-v\,w)-\frac{vw(1+v-2vw)}{(1-v)^{3}}\,\ln(1-v+v\,w)

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt+\Bigg(-\frac{v}{(1-v)^{2}\,w}-\frac{4v^{2}-v+1}{(1-v)^{3}}+\frac{v(7v+3)w}{(1-v)^{3}}-\frac{6v^{2}w^{2}}{(1-v)^{3}}\Bigg)\,\ln(1-w)

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt+\frac{-6v^{3}w^{2}+v^{2}\left(6w^{2}-2w-1\right)+2vw-1}{(1-v)^{4}}\,\ln w+\frac{8v^{2}w^{2}}{(1-v)^{4}}

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt-\frac{v^{3}+v^{2}-5v+1}{(1-v)^{4}}-\frac{v(6v+5)w}{(1-v)^{4}}+\frac{1}{(1-v)\,(1-v+v\,w)}\Bigg]\,.

D.3
Channel
qq\to q^{\prime}

D.3.1
Integral Contribution

For the integral contribution to the
qq\to q^{\prime}
-channel in Eq. (
73
) we found the following partonic factors,

\displaystyle\hat{\sigma}_{\mathrm{Int},1}^{qq\to q^{\prime},1}(v,w,\zeta)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{v\,w}{2\,(1-v)^{4}}\Bigg(\frac{(2\zeta-1)(1-v)^{2}(1+v(w-\zeta))}{\sqrt{1-2\zeta v+2(2\zeta-1)vw+v^{2}(w-\zeta)^{2}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt+\frac{1}{\zeta}\text{sgn}(w-\zeta)\left(\zeta(2\zeta-1)+v^{2}\left(\zeta(4\zeta-1)+4w^{2}-6\zeta w\right)+2\zeta v(w-\zeta)\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt+\frac{2v}{\zeta}\Big(2(\zeta-1)\zeta v^{2}w\left(6\zeta+2(6\zeta-1)w^{2}+(2-16\zeta)w-1\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt+v\left(\zeta^{2}+\left(8\zeta^{2}-4\zeta-2\right)w^{2}+\zeta\left(-4\zeta^{2}-2\zeta+3\right)w\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt+\zeta\left(\zeta+\left(4\zeta^{2}-6\zeta+3\right)w-1\right)\Big)\Bigg)\,,

\displaystyle\hat{\sigma}_{\mathrm{Int},2}^{qq\to q^{\prime},1}(v,w,\zeta)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\hat{\sigma}_{\mathrm{Int},1}^{qq\to q^{\prime},1}(v,w,1-\zeta)\,,

\displaystyle\hat{\sigma}_{\mathrm{Int}}^{qq\to q^{\prime},1}(v,w,\zeta)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\hat{\sigma}_{\mathrm{Int},1}^{qq\to q^{\prime},1}(v,w,\zeta)+\hat{\sigma}_{\mathrm{Int},2}^{qq\to q^{\prime},1}(v,w,\zeta)\,.

(186)

\displaystyle\hat{\sigma}_{\mathrm{Int},1}^{qq\to q^{\prime},5}(v,w,\zeta)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{v\,w}{2\,(1-v)^{4}}\Bigg(-\frac{(1-v)^{2}(1+v(w-\zeta))}{\sqrt{1-2\zeta v+2(2\zeta-1)vw+v^{2}(w-\zeta)^{2}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt-\frac{1}{\zeta}\text{sgn}(w-\zeta)\left(\zeta+v^{2}\left(2\zeta^{2}+\zeta+4w^{2}-6\zeta w\right)+2\zeta v(w-\zeta)\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt-\frac{2v}{\zeta}\Big(-2(1-\zeta)\zeta v^{2}w\left(2w^{2}-2w+1\right)+v\left(\zeta^{2}+(4\zeta-2)w^{2}+3(1-2\zeta)\zeta w\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt+\zeta(-\zeta+(6\zeta-5)w+1)\Big)\Bigg)\,,

\displaystyle\hat{\sigma}_{\mathrm{Int},2}^{qq\to q^{\prime},5}(v,w,\zeta)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle+\hat{\sigma}_{\mathrm{Int},1}^{qq\to q^{\prime},5}(v,w,1-\zeta)\,,

\displaystyle\hat{\sigma}_{\mathrm{Int}}^{qq\to q^{\prime},5}(v,w,\zeta)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\hat{\sigma}_{\mathrm{Int},1}^{qq\to q^{\prime},5}(v,w,\zeta)+\hat{\sigma}_{\mathrm{Int},2}^{qq\to q^{\prime},5}(v,w,\zeta)\,.

(187)

D.3.2
Soft-Fermion Pole Contribution

The two partonic factors in our formula for the soft-fermion contribution to the
qq\to q^{\prime}
-channel Eq.
74
read explicitly,

\displaystyle\hat{\sigma}_{\mathrm{SFP},F}^{qq\to q^{\prime},1}(v,w,\chi_{m})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\Bigg[\Big(\frac{4v^{4}w^{4}}{(1-v)^{4}}-\frac{4v^{3}(v+1)w^{3}}{(1-v)^{4}}+\frac{2v^{2}\left(v^{2}+2\right)w^{2}}{(1-v)^{4}}

(188)

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt-\frac{2v^{2}w}{(1-v)^{4}}+\frac{1}{(1-v)^{2}}-\frac{1}{(1-v)^{2}(1-v\,w)}\Big)\,\ln\chi_{m}+\frac{w\left(4vw^{2}+v-4w\right)}{(1-v)^{2}\,(1-v\,w)}\,\ln(1-v)

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt-\frac{vw(1+v\,w)}{(1-v)^{2}\,(1-v\,w)}\,\ln(1-v\,w)-\frac{v\,w}{(1-v)^{2}}\,\ln(1-v+v\,w)

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt+\Big(\frac{4v^{4}w^{4}}{(1-v)^{4}}-\frac{4v^{4}w^{3}}{(1-v)^{4}}+\frac{2\left(v^{2}-3v+3\right)v^{2}w^{2}}{(1-v)^{4}}+\frac{(1-3v)vw}{(1-v)^{3}}+\frac{1}{(1-v)^{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt-\frac{1}{(1-v)^{2}\,(1-v\,w)}\Big)\,\ln(1-w)+\Big(-\frac{4v^{4}w^{4}}{(1-v)^{4}}+\frac{4v^{4}w^{3}}{(1-v)^{4}}-\frac{2\left(v^{2}+3v+1\right)v^{2}w^{2}}{(1-v)^{4}}

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt-\frac{vw}{(1-v)^{2}}-\frac{1}{(1-v)^{2}}+\frac{1}{(1-v)^{2}(1-v\,w)}\Big)\,\ln w-\frac{12v^{4}w^{4}}{(1-v)^{4}}+\frac{12v^{3}(v+2)w^{3}}{(1-v)^{4}}

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt-\frac{2v^{2}\left(2v^{2}+8v+1\right)w^{2}}{(1-v)^{4}}+\frac{2v^{2}w}{(1-v)^{4}}\Bigg]\,,

\displaystyle\hat{\sigma}_{\mathrm{SFP},G}^{qq\to q^{\prime},5}(v,w,\chi_{m})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\Bigg[\Big(\frac{4v^{4}w^{4}}{(1-v)^{4}}-\frac{4v^{3}(v+1)w^{3}}{(1-v)^{4}}+\frac{2v^{2}\left(v^{2}+2\right)w^{2}}{(1-v)^{4}}

(189)

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt-\frac{2v^{2}w}{(1-v)^{4}}+\frac{1}{(1-v)^{2}}-\frac{1}{(1-v)^{2}(1-v\,w)}\Big)\,\ln\chi_{m}+\frac{v\,w}{(1-v)^{2}\,(1-v\,w)}\,\ln(1-v)

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt-\frac{vw(1+v\,w)}{(1-v)^{2}\,(1-v\,w)}\,\ln(1-v\,w)-\frac{v\,w}{(1-v)^{2}}\,\ln(1-v+v\,w)

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt+\Big(\frac{4v^{4}w^{4}}{(1-v)^{4}}-\frac{4v^{4}w^{3}}{(1-v)^{4}}+\frac{2\left(v^{2}-3v+3\right)v^{2}w^{2}}{(1-v)^{4}}+\frac{(1-3v)vw}{(1-v)^{3}}+\frac{1}{(1-v)^{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt-\frac{1}{(1-v)^{2}\,(1-v\,w)}\Big)\,\ln(1-w)+\Big(-\frac{4v^{4}w^{4}}{(1-v)^{4}}+\frac{4v^{4}w^{3}}{(1-v)^{4}}-\frac{2\left(v^{2}-3v+3\right)v^{2}w^{2}}{(1-v)^{4}}

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt-\frac{vw}{(1-v)^{2}}-\frac{1}{(1-v)^{2}}+\frac{1}{(1-v)^{2}(1-v\,w)}\Big)\,\ln w-\frac{4v^{4}w^{4}}{(1-v)^{4}}+\frac{4v^{4}w^{3}}{(1-v)^{4}}

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt-\frac{2v^{2}w^{2}}{(1-v)^{4}}+\frac{2v^{2}w}{(1-v)^{4}}\Bigg]\,.

D.4
Channel
qg\to g

D.4.1
Integral Contribution

For the
qg\to g
-channel we again start with the partonic factors of the integral contribution, cf. Eq. (
76
). As for the
qg\to q
-channel we encounter two color factors,
C_{F}
and
N_{c}
. We can split the results according to these two factors. For the integral contributions, we write

\displaystyle\hat{\sigma}_{\mathrm{Int}}^{qg\to g,1}(v,w,\zeta)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C_{F}\,\hat{\sigma}_{\mathrm{Int},C_{F}}^{qg\to g,1}(v,w,\zeta)+N_{c}\,\hat{\sigma}_{\mathrm{Int},N_{c}}^{qg\to g,1}(v,w,\zeta)\,,

(190)

with

\displaystyle\hat{\sigma}_{\mathrm{Int},C_{F}}^{qg\to g,1}(v,w,\zeta)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{v\,w}{2\,(1-v)^{4}}\Bigg(-\zeta(1-2v-v^{2})-2(1-\zeta)\zeta v^{3}w\left(6w^{2}-8w+3\right)

(191)

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt-2\left(4\zeta^{2}-6\zeta+\tfrac{1}{\zeta}\right)v^{2}\,w^{2}+4\left(\zeta^{2}-3\zeta+1\right)v^{2}\,w+2\left(\zeta^{2}+\zeta-2\right)v\,w+2v

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt+\frac{(1+\zeta)(1-v)^{2}(1+v(w-\zeta))}{\sqrt{1-2\zeta v+2(2\zeta-1)vw+v^{2}(w-\zeta)^{2}}}+\text{sgn}(w-\zeta)\left(1+v^{2}\left(1+\frac{2(w-\zeta)^{2}}{\zeta}\right)\right)\Bigg)\,,

\displaystyle\hat{\sigma}_{\mathrm{Int},N_{c}}^{qg\to g,1}(v,w,\zeta)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{vw}{4\,(1-v)^{4}}\Bigg(-(2+\zeta)-v^{2}\left(\zeta+\left(4\zeta-\tfrac{2}{\zeta}\right)w^{2}+2(1-2\zeta)w\right)

(192)

\displaystyle-2(1-2\zeta)vw-\text{sgn}(w-\zeta)\,\left(1+v^{2}+v^{2}\left(2\,\zeta+2\tfrac{w^{2}}{\zeta}-4\,w\right)\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt-\frac{(1-v)}{\left[1-2\zeta v+2(2\zeta-1)vw+v^{2}(w-\zeta)^{2}\right]^{\tfrac{3}{2}}}\Big[-1-\zeta+v^{4}(w-\zeta)^{3}(-\zeta+2w-1)

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt+v^{3}\left(\zeta^{2}\left(\zeta^{2}-2\zeta-3\right)+(11\zeta-7)w^{3}+\left(-19\zeta^{2}+4\zeta+3\right)w^{2}+\zeta^{2}(7\zeta+5)w\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt+(\zeta-1)v^{2}\left(-3\zeta(\zeta+1)+(14\zeta-5)w^{2}+\left(2\zeta^{2}-2\zeta+1\right)w\right)

\displaystyle+v\left(3\zeta^{2}+2\zeta+\left(-6\zeta^{2}+\zeta+1\right)w-1\right)\Big]\Bigg)\,,

and

\displaystyle\hat{\sigma}_{\mathrm{Int}}^{qg\to g,5}(v,w,\zeta)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C_{F}\,\hat{\sigma}_{\mathrm{Int},C_{F}}^{qg\to g,5}(v,w,\zeta)+N_{c}\,\hat{\sigma}_{\mathrm{Int},N_{c}}^{qg\to g,5}(v,w,\zeta)\,,

(193)

with

\displaystyle\hat{\sigma}_{\mathrm{Int},C_{F}}^{qg\to g,5}(v,w,\zeta)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{v\,w}{2\,(1-v)^{4}}\Bigg(\zeta(1-2v-v^{2})+2(1-\zeta)\zeta v^{3}w\left(6w^{2}-8w+3\right)

(194)

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt+2\left(4\zeta^{2}-6\zeta+\tfrac{1}{\zeta}\right)v^{2}\,w^{2}-4\left(\zeta^{2}-3\zeta+1\right)v^{2}\,w-2(1-\zeta)(2-\zeta)v\,w+2v

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt+\frac{(1-\zeta)(1-v)^{2}(1+v(w-\zeta))}{\sqrt{1-2\zeta v+2(2\zeta-1)vw+v^{2}(w-\zeta)^{2}}}+\text{sgn}(w-\zeta)\left(1+v^{2}-v^{2}\left(2\zeta+\tfrac{2w^{2}}{\zeta}-4w\right)\right)\Bigg)\,,

\displaystyle\hat{\sigma}_{\mathrm{Int},N_{c}}^{qg\to g,5}(v,w,\zeta)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{v\,w}{4\,(1-v)^{4}}\Bigg(-(2-\zeta)+v^{2}\left(\zeta+\left(4\zeta-\tfrac{2}{\zeta}\right)w^{2}+2(1-2\zeta)w\right)

(195)

\displaystyle+2(1-2\zeta)vw-\text{sgn}(w-\zeta)\left(1+v^{2}-v^{2}\,\left(2\,\zeta+\tfrac{2\,w^{2}}{\zeta}-4\,w\right)\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt+\frac{(1-v)}{\left[1-2\zeta v+2(2\zeta-1)vw+v^{2}(w-\zeta)^{2}\right]^{\tfrac{3}{2}}}\Big[1-\zeta+v^{4}(w-\zeta)^{3}(-\zeta+2w+1)

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt+v^{3}\left(\zeta^{2}\left(\zeta^{2}-4\zeta+3\right)+(11\zeta-5)w^{3}+\left(-19\zeta^{2}+10\zeta-3\right)w^{2}+\zeta^{2}(7\zeta-1)w\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt+v^{2}\left(-3(\zeta-1)^{2}\zeta+\left(14\zeta^{2}-15\zeta+7\right)w^{2}+\left(2\zeta^{3}-8\zeta^{2}-\zeta+1\right)w\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt+v\left(3\zeta^{2}-4\zeta+\left(-6\zeta^{2}+13\zeta-5\right)w+1\right)\Big]\Bigg)\,.

D.4.2
Soft-Gluon Pole Contribution

Next, we state our results for the partonic factors appearing in Eq. (
77
), the soft-gluon pole contribution to the
qg\to g
-channel. This contribution can again be separated according to its color structures,

\hat{\sigma}_{\mathrm{SGP},F}^{qg\to g,1}(v,w,\chi_{\mu},\chi_{m})=C_{F}\,\hat{\sigma}_{\mathrm{SGP},F,C_{F}}^{qg\to g,1}(v,w,\chi_{\mu})+N_{c}\,\hat{\sigma}_{\mathrm{SGP},F,N_{c}}^{qg\to g,1}(v,w,\chi_{m})\,,

(196)

with

\displaystyle\hat{\sigma}_{\mathrm{SGP},F,C_{F}}^{qg\to g,1}(v,w,\chi_{\mu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle A_{1,\mathrm{SGP},F,C_{F}}^{qg\to g,1}(v,\chi_{\mu})\,\delta(1-w)

(197)

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt+A_{2,\mathrm{SGP},F,C_{F}}^{qg\to g,1}(v)\frac{1}{(1-w)_{+}}+A_{3,\mathrm{SGP},F,C_{F}}^{qg\to g,1}(v,w)\,\ln(\chi_{\mu})

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt+A_{4,\mathrm{SGP},F,C_{F}}^{qg\to g,1}(v,w)\,\ln(1-w)+A_{5,\mathrm{SGP},F,C_{F}}^{qg\to g,1}(v,w)\,\ln(1-v)

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt+A_{6,\mathrm{SGP},F,C_{F}}^{qg\to g,1}(v,w)\,\ln(1-v+v\,w)+A_{7,\mathrm{SGP},F,C_{F}}^{qg\to g,1}(v,w)\,\ln(w)

\displaystyle+A_{8,\mathrm{SGP},F,C_{F}}^{qg\to g,1}(v,w)\,.

The eight coefficients read,

\displaystyle A_{1,\mathrm{SGP},F,C_{F}}^{qg\to g,1}(v,\chi_{\mu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{v\left(1+v^{2}\right)}{(1-v)^{4}}\ln\chi_{\mu}+\frac{2v^{3}+5v^{2}+1}{2\,(1-v)^{4}}\ln(1-v)-\frac{v^{4}-8v^{3}+9v^{2}-6v}{4\,(1-v)^{4}}\,,

\displaystyle A_{2,\mathrm{SGP},F,C_{F}}^{qg\to g,1}(v)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{3\,v\,\left(1+v^{2}\right)}{2\,(1-v)^{4}}\,,

\displaystyle A_{3,\mathrm{SGP},F,C_{F}}^{qg\to g,1}(v,w)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{6v^{4}w^{4}}{(1-v)^{4}}+\frac{8v^{4}w^{3}}{(1-v)^{4}}-\frac{\left(3v^{2}-2v+3\right)v^{2}w^{2}}{(1-v)^{4}}+\frac{2}{(1-v)^{2}}+\frac{v(2-4w)-2}{(1-v)\,(1-v+v\,w)^{2}}\,,

\displaystyle A_{4,\mathrm{SGP},F,C_{F}}^{qg\to g,1}(v,w)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{6v^{4}w^{4}}{(1-v)^{4}}+\frac{v^{3}(8v-1)w^{3}}{(1-v)^{4}}-\frac{v^{2}\left(3v^{2}-2v+3\right)w^{2}}{(1-v)^{4}}-\frac{v(v+1)w}{2(1-v)^{3}}

\displaystyle+\frac{2}{(1-v)^{2}}+\frac{v(2-4w)-2}{(1-v)\,(1-v+v\,w)^{2}}\,,

\displaystyle A_{5,\mathrm{SGP},F,C_{F}}^{qg\to g,1}(v,w)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{12v^{4}w^{4}}{(1-v)^{4}}+\frac{16v^{4}w^{3}}{(1-v)^{4}}-\frac{2\left(3v^{2}-2v+3\right)v^{2}w^{2}}{(1-v)^{4}}+\frac{2}{(1-v)^{2}}+\frac{v(2-4w)-2}{(1-v)\,(1-v+v\,w)^{2}}\,,

\displaystyle A_{6,\mathrm{SGP},F,C_{F}}^{qg\to g,1}(v,w)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{4v^{2}w^{2}}{(1-v)^{2}\,(1-v+v\,w)^{2}}\,,

\displaystyle A_{7,\mathrm{SGP},F,C_{F}}^{qg\to g,1}(v,w)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{6v^{4}w^{4}}{(1-v)^{4}}-\frac{v^{3}(8v-1)w^{3}}{(1-v)^{4}}+\frac{v^{2}\left(3v^{2}-2v+3\right)w^{2}}{(1-v)^{4}}-\frac{2}{(1-v)^{2}}+\frac{v(4w-2)+2}{(1-v)\,(1-v+v\,w)^{2}}\,,

\displaystyle A_{8,\mathrm{SGP},F,C_{F}}^{qg\to g,1}(v,w)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{3v^{4}w^{4}}{(1-v)^{4}}-\frac{2v^{3}(8+v)w^{3}}{(1-v)^{4}}+\frac{v^{2}\left(11v-2\right)w^{2}}{(1-v)^{4}}+\frac{3v\left(v^{2}-4v+1\right)w}{2(1-v)^{4}}

(198)

\displaystyle+\frac{3v^{3}-26v^{2}+31v-14}{2(1-v)^{4}}+\frac{11-3v}{(1-v)(1-v+v\,w)}-\frac{4}{(1-v+v\,w)^{2}}\,.

The
N_{c}
-part reads

\displaystyle\hat{\sigma}_{\mathrm{SGP},F,N_{c}}^{qg\to g,1}(v,w,\chi_{m})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle A_{1,\mathrm{SGP},F,N_{c}}^{qg\to g,1}(v,\chi_{m})\,\delta(1-w)

(199)

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt+A_{2,\mathrm{SGP},F,N_{c}}^{qg\to g,1}(v)\frac{1}{(1-w)_{+}}+A_{3,\mathrm{SGP},F,N_{c}}^{qg\to g,1}(v,w)\,\ln(\chi_{m})

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt+A_{4,\mathrm{SGP},F,N_{c}}^{qg\to g,1}(v,w)\,\ln(1-w)+A_{5,\mathrm{SGP},F,N_{c}}^{qg\to g,1}(v,w)\,\ln(1-v)

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt+A_{6,\mathrm{SGP},F,N_{c}}^{qg\to g,1}(v,w)\,\ln(1-v\,w)+A_{7,\mathrm{SGP},F,N_{c}}^{qg\to g,1}(v,w)\,\ln(w)

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt+A_{8,\mathrm{SGP},F,N_{c}}^{qg\to g,1}(v,w)\,,

with the eight coefficients

\displaystyle A_{1,\mathrm{SGP},F,N_{c}}^{qg\to g,1}(v,\chi_{m})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{v^{3}-2v^{2}+2v}{2\,(1-v)^{4}}\ln(\chi_{m})+\frac{1-2v}{2\,(1-v)^{4}}\ln(1-v)-\frac{(1+v)v^{2}}{4\,(1-v)^{4}}\,,

\displaystyle A_{2,\mathrm{SGP},F,N_{c}}^{qg\to g,1}(v)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{v\,(3-v)}{4\,(1-v)^{3}}\,,

\displaystyle A_{3,\mathrm{SGP},F,N_{c}}^{qg\to g,1}(v,w)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{2v^{3}w^{3}}{(1-v)^{4}}+\frac{v^{2}(v+1)w^{2}}{(1-v)^{4}}+\frac{1}{(1-v)^{3}}-\frac{3-v}{(1-v)^{3}\,(1-v\,w)}

\displaystyle+\frac{3-2v}{(1-v)^{3}(1-v\,w)^{2}}-\frac{1}{(1-v)^{2}\,(1-v\,w)^{3}}\,,

\displaystyle A_{4,\mathrm{SGP},F,N_{c}}^{qg\to g,1}(v,w)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{3v^{3}w^{3}}{2\,(1-v)^{4}}+\frac{v^{2}(v+1)w^{2}}{(1-v)^{4}}+\frac{v(1+v)w}{4\,(1-v)^{3}}-\frac{3-v}{(1-v)^{3}\,(1-v\,w)}

\displaystyle+\frac{3-2v}{(1-v)^{3}\,(1-v\,w)^{2}}-\frac{1}{(1-v)^{2}\,(1-v\,w)^{3}}+\frac{1}{(1-v)^{3}}\,,

\displaystyle A_{5,\mathrm{SGP},F,N_{c}}^{qg\to g,1}(v,w)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{v^{3}(1-w)w^{2}}{(1-v)^{3}\,(1-v\,w)^{3}}\,,

\displaystyle A_{6,\mathrm{SGP},F,N_{c}}^{qg\to g,1}(v,w)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2v^{3}(1-w)w^{2}}{(1-v)^{3}\,(1-v\,w)^{3}}\,,

\displaystyle A_{7,\mathrm{SGP},F,N_{c}}^{qg\to g,1}(v,w)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{3v^{3}w^{3}}{2\,(1-v)^{4}}-\frac{v^{2}(1+v)w^{2}}{(1-v)^{4}}+\frac{3-v}{(1-v)^{3}\,(1-v\,w)}

\displaystyle-\frac{3-2v}{(1-v)^{3}(1-v\,w)^{2}}+\frac{1}{(1-v)^{2}(1-v\,w)^{3}}-\frac{1}{(1-v)^{3}}\,,

\displaystyle A_{8,\mathrm{SGP},F,N_{c}}^{qg\to g,1}(v,w)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{3v^{3}w^{3}}{2\,(1-v)^{4}}-\frac{v^{2}(1+2v)w^{2}}{2\,(1-v)^{4}}-\frac{v\left(1+v^{2}\right)w}{4(1-v)^{4}}+\frac{8v^{2}-32v+25}{2\,(1-v)^{4}\,(1-v\,w)}

(200)

\displaystyle-\frac{v^{3}+4v^{2}-29v+26}{4(1-v)^{4}}+\frac{2}{(1-v)\,(1-v+v\,w)}

\displaystyle-\frac{21-13v}{2\,(1-v)^{3}\,(1-v\,w)^{2}}-\frac{1}{2\,(1-v+v\,w)^{2}}+\frac{3}{(1-v)^{2}\,(1-v\,w)^{3}}\,.

The other SGP contribution entering with the axial-vector
qgq
correlation function has a relatively simple expression:

\displaystyle\hat{\sigma}_{\mathrm{SGP},\partial_{2}G}^{qg\to g,5}(v,w)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{v(2C_{F}-N_{c})\left(v^{2}\left(2w^{2}-4w+1\right)-1\right)}{2\,(1-v)^{4}}\ln(1-w)

(201)

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt-\frac{w\left(C_{F}(1-v)+N_{c}\left(1-v+v^{2}\right)\right)}{(1-v)^{3}}\ln(1-v)-\frac{v^{3}w^{2}(2C_{F}-N_{c})}{(1-v)^{4}}\ln(w)

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt+\frac{N_{c}v^{2}w}{(1-v)^{3}}\ln(1-v+v\,w)+C_{F}\,\frac{v\,w\left(v^{3}\left(6w^{2}-8w+3\right)-4v^{2}+v-2\right)}{2(1-v)^{4}}

\displaystyle+N_{c}\,\frac{v\,w\,\left(v^{2}(1-2w)+3v-2\right)}{2\,(1-v)^{4}}\,.

D.4.3
Soft-Fermion Pole Contribution

The explicit result for the partonic functions of the soft-fermion pole contribution to the
qg\to g
-channel in Eq. (
78
) can be split up according to its color factors,

\hat{\sigma}_{\mathrm{SFP},F}^{qg\to g,1}(v,w,\chi_{m})=C_{F}\,\hat{\sigma}_{\mathrm{SFP},F,C_{F}}^{qg\to g,1}(v,w,\chi_{m})+N_{c}\,\hat{\sigma}_{\mathrm{SFP},F,N_{c}}^{qg\to g,1}(v,w,\chi_{m})\,,

(202)

with

\displaystyle\hat{\sigma}_{\mathrm{SFP},F,C_{F}}^{qg\to g,1}(v,w,\chi_{m})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle A_{1,\mathrm{SFP},F,C_{F}}^{qg\to g,1}(v,w)\,\ln(\chi_{m})

(203)

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt+A_{2,\mathrm{SFP},F,C_{F}}^{qg\to g,1}(v,w)\,\ln(1-w)+A_{3,\mathrm{SFP},F,C_{F}}^{qg\to g,1}(v,w)\,\ln(1-v)

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt+A_{4,\mathrm{SFP},F,C_{F}}^{qg\to g,1}(v,w)\,\ln(1-v+v\,w)+A_{5,\mathrm{SFP},F,C_{F}}^{qg\to g,1}(v,w)\,\ln(1-v\,w)

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt+A_{6,\mathrm{SFP},F,C_{F}}^{qg\to g,1}(v,w)\,\ln(w)+A_{7,\mathrm{SFP},F,C_{F}}^{qg\to g,1}(v,w)\,,

with the seven coefficients

\displaystyle A_{1,\mathrm{SFP},F,C_{F}}^{qg\to g,1}(v,w)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{v^{2}w^{2}\left(v^{2}\left(2w^{2}-2w+1\right)-2vw+1\right)}{(1-v)^{4}\,(1-v\,w)}\,,

\displaystyle A_{2,\mathrm{SFP},F,C_{F}}^{qg\to g,1}(v,w)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{v^{2}w\left(v^{2}w(2w-1)+v(2-4w)+w\right)}{(1-v)^{4}\,(1-v\,w)}\,,

\displaystyle A_{3,\mathrm{SFP},F,C_{F}}^{qg\to g,1}(v,w)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{w\left(2v^{3}w(2w-1)+v^{2}\left(w^{2}-4w+1\right)-v\left(w^{2}+w-1\right)+w\right)}{(1-v)^{3}\,(1-v\,w)}\,,

\displaystyle A_{4,\mathrm{SFP},F,C_{F}}^{qg\to g,1}(v,w)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{v\,w\,(1+v(1-2w))}{(1-v)^{3}}\,,

\displaystyle A_{5,\mathrm{SFP},F,C_{F}}^{qg\to g,1}(v,w)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{v\,w\,\left(v^{2}w(2w-1)-v(w+1)+1\right)}{(1-v)^{3}\,(1-v\,w)}\,,

\displaystyle A_{6,\mathrm{SFP},F,C_{F}}^{qg\to g,1}(v,w)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{v^{2}\,w^{2}\,\left(v^{2}(6w-1)-4v(w+1)+3\right)}{(1-v)^{4}\,(1-v\,w)}\,,

\displaystyle A_{7,\mathrm{SFP},F,C_{F}}^{qg\to g,1}(v,w)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{3v^{4}w^{4}}{(1-v)^{4}}+\frac{2v^{3}(2v+5)w^{3}}{(1-v)^{4}}-\frac{v\left(3v^{3}+20v^{2}-3v-2\right)w^{2}}{2(1-v)^{4}}

(204)

\displaystyle-\frac{vw}{(1-v)^{3}}-\frac{1}{(1-v)(1-v+v\,w)}+\frac{1}{(1-v)^{2}}\,.

The
N_{c}
part reads,

\displaystyle\hat{\sigma}_{\mathrm{SFP},F,N_{c}}^{qg\to g,1}(v,w,\chi_{m})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle A_{1,\mathrm{SFP},F,N_{c}}^{qg\to g,1}(v,w)\,\ln(\chi_{m})

(205)

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt+A_{2,\mathrm{SFP},F,N_{c}}^{qg\to g,1}(v,w)\,\ln(1-w)+A_{3,\mathrm{SFP},F,N_{c}}^{qg\to g,1}(v,w)\,\ln(1-v)

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt+A_{4,\mathrm{SFP},F,N_{c}}^{qg\to g,1}(v,w)\,\ln(1-v+v\,w)+A_{5,\mathrm{SFP},F,N_{c}}^{qg\to g,1}(v,w)\,\ln(1-v\,w)

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt+A_{6,\mathrm{SFP},F,N_{c}}^{qg\to g,1}(v,w)\,\ln(w)+A_{7,\mathrm{SFP},F,N_{c}}^{qg\to g,1}(v,w)\,,

with the seven coefficients

\displaystyle A_{1,\mathrm{SFP},F,N_{c}}^{qg\to g,1}(v,w)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{v^{2}w^{2}\left(v^{3}w\left(2w^{2}-2w+1\right)+v^{2}\left(-6w^{2}+4w-2\right)+5vw-2\right)}{2\,(1-v)^{4}\,(1-v\,w)^{2}}\,,

\displaystyle A_{2,\mathrm{SFP},F,N_{c}}^{qg\to g,1}(v,w)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{v^{2}w\left(v^{3}w^{2}(2w-1)-2v^{2}w\left(w^{2}+2w-1\right)+v\left(3w^{2}+4w-2\right)-2w\right)}{2\,(1-v)^{4}\,(1-v\,w)^{2}}\,,

\displaystyle A_{3,\mathrm{SFP},F,N_{c}}^{qg\to g,1}(v,w)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{\left(3v^{2}-v+1\right)w^{2}}{(1-v)^{3}}+\frac{v(1+v)w}{(1-v)^{3}}-\frac{3-v}{2\,(1-v)^{3}\,(1-v\,w)}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2\,(1-v)^{2}\,(1-v\,w)^{2}}+\frac{1}{(1-v)^{3}}\,,

\displaystyle A_{4,\mathrm{SFP},F,N_{c}}^{qg\to g,1}(v,w)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{v\,w\,(1+v(1-2w))}{2\,(1-v)^{3}}\,,

\displaystyle A_{5,\mathrm{SFP},F,N_{c}}^{qg\to g,1}(v,w)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{v\,w\,\left(v^{3}w^{2}(4w-1)+v^{2}(2-9w)w+2vw+v+1\right)}{2\,(1-v)^{3}\,(1-v\,w)^{2}}\,,

\displaystyle A_{6,\mathrm{SFP},F,N_{c}}^{qg\to g,1}(v,w)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{v^{2}w^{2}\left(v^{3}w(6w-1)-2v^{2}\left(w^{2}+6w-1\right)+v(5w+4)-2\right)}{2\,(1-v)^{4}\,(1-v\,w)^{2}}\,,

\displaystyle A_{7,\mathrm{SFP},F,N_{c}}^{qg\to g,1}(v,w)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{4v^{3}w^{3}}{(1-v)^{4}}+\frac{v\left(7v^{2}+7v-4\right)w^{2}}{2\,(1-v)^{4}}+\frac{2vw}{(1-v)^{3}}+\frac{4-v}{2\,(1-v)^{3}}

(206)

\displaystyle-\frac{7-4v}{2\,(1-v)^{3}\,(1-v\,w)}+\frac{1}{2\,(1-v)\,(1-v+v\,w)}+\frac{1}{(1-v)^{2}\,(1-v\,w)^{2}}\,.

The result for the partonic functions for the axial-vector soft-fermion pole contributions can also be split up according to its color factors,

\hat{\sigma}_{\mathrm{SFP},G}^{qg\to g,5}(v,w,\chi_{m})=C_{F}\,\hat{\sigma}_{\mathrm{SFP},G,C_{F}}^{qg\to g,5}(v,w,\chi_{m})+N_{c}\,\hat{\sigma}_{\mathrm{SFP},G,N_{c}}^{qg\to g,5}(v,w,\chi_{m})\,.

(207)

The
\hat{\sigma}_{\mathrm{SFP},G}^{qg\to g,5}
are quite similar to the
\hat{\sigma}_{\mathrm{SFP},F}^{qg\to g,1}
. Indeed, we find,

\displaystyle\hat{\sigma}_{\mathrm{SFP},G,C_{F}}^{qg\to g,5}(v,w,\chi_{m})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\hat{\sigma}_{\mathrm{SFP},F,C_{F}}^{qg\to g,1}(v,w,\chi_{m})+\frac{2\left(1-v-2v^{2}\right)w^{2}}{(1-v)^{3}}\ln(1-v)

(208)

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt+\frac{4v^{2}\,w^{2}}{(1-v)^{3}}\,\ln(1-v\,w)-\frac{4v^{2}(1-3v)w^{2}}{(1-v)^{4}}\,\ln(w)

\displaystyle+\frac{v^{2}\,w^{2}\,\left(v^{2}\left(6w^{2}-8w+3\right)-16v(w-1)-1\right)}{(1-v)^{4}}\,,

and

\displaystyle\hat{\sigma}_{\mathrm{SFP},G,N_{c}}^{qg\to g,5}(v,w,\chi_{m})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\hat{\sigma}_{\mathrm{SFP},F,N_{c}}^{qg\to g,1}(v,w,\chi_{m})+\frac{2\left(1-v+2v^{2}\right)w^{2}}{(1-v)^{3}}\ln(1-v)

(209)

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt-\frac{4v^{2}w^{2}}{(1-v)^{3}}\,\ln(1-v\,w)+\frac{2(1-3v)v^{2}w^{2}}{(1-v)^{4}}\,\ln(w)+\frac{vw^{2}\left(v^{2}(6w-5)-4v+3\right)}{(1-v)^{4}}\,.

D.5
Channel
gg\to q^{\prime}

We conclude our explicit results for hadron production with the two partonic factors of Eq. (
79
), our formula for the
gg\to q^{\prime}
-channel. They are given as

\displaystyle\hat{\sigma}_{xx}^{gg\to q^{\prime}}(v,w,\chi_{m},\chi_{\mu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle A_{1,xx}^{gg\to q^{\prime}}(v,\chi_{m},\chi_{\mu})\delta(1-w)+A_{2,xx}^{gg\to q^{\prime}}(v)\frac{1}{(1-w)_{+}}

(210)

\displaystyle+A_{3,xx}^{gg\to q^{\prime}}(v,w)\ln(\chi_{m})+A_{4,xx}^{gg\to q^{\prime}}(v,w)\ln(1-w)

\displaystyle+A_{5,xx}^{gg\to q^{\prime}}(v,w)\ln(\chi_{\mu})+A_{6,xx}^{gg\to q^{\prime}}(v,w)\ln(1-v\,w)

\displaystyle+A_{7,xx}^{gg\to q^{\prime}}(v,w)\ln(1-v)+A_{8,xx}^{gg\to q^{\prime}}(v,w)\ln(w)+A_{9,xx}^{gg\to q^{\prime}}(v,w)\,

with the following nine coefficient functions:

\displaystyle A_{1,xx}^{gg\to q^{\prime}}(v,\chi_{m},\chi_{\mu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2(v-1)v+1}{(1-v)^{4}}\ln(\chi_{m})+\frac{1+v^{2}}{(1-v)^{4}}\ln(\chi_{\mu})+\frac{v^{2}-(1-v)^{2}}{(1-v)^{4}}

\displaystyle+\frac{(3v-2)v+2}{(1-v)^{4}}\ln(1-v)\,,

\displaystyle A_{2,xx}^{gg\to q^{\prime}}(v)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2-2v+3v^{2}}{(1-v)^{4}}\,,

\displaystyle A_{3,xx}^{gg\to q^{\prime}}(v,w)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2v\left(-2vw^{2}+v-2\right)+4}{(1-v)^{4}}-\frac{4(2-v)v^{2}w^{2}+(3-v)(1-3vw)}{(1-v)^{3}(1-v\,w)^{3}}\,,

\displaystyle A_{4,xx}^{gg\to q^{\prime}}(v,w)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{-2\left(3v^{2}+1\right)w^{2}+3v^{2}-4v+5}{(1-v)^{4}}-\frac{4(2-v)v^{2}w^{2}+(3-v)(1-3vw)}{(1-v)^{3}(1-v\,w)^{3}}\,,

\displaystyle A_{5,xx}^{gg\to q^{\prime}}(v,w)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\left(1+v^{2}\right)\left(1-2w^{2}\right)}{(1-v)^{4}}\,,

\displaystyle A_{6,xx}^{gg\to q^{\prime}}(v,w)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2v^{2}w^{2}((v-3)vw+v+1)}{(1-v)^{3}(1-v\,w)^{3}}\,,

\displaystyle A_{7,xx}^{gg\to q^{\prime}}(v,w)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{v\left(-6vw^{2}+v+4\right)-1-2w^{2}}{(1-v)^{4}}+\frac{4(2-v)v^{2}w^{2}+(3-v)(1-3vw)}{(1-v)^{3}(1-v\,w)^{3}}\,,

\displaystyle A_{8,xx}^{gg\to q^{\prime}}(v,w)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2(v+1)w^{2}+v-3}{(1-v)^{3}}+\frac{4(2-v)v^{2}w^{2}+(3-v)(1-3vw)}{(1-v)^{3}(1-v\,w)^{3}}\,,

\displaystyle A_{9,xx}^{gg\to q^{\prime}}(v,w)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{8v^{2}w-2(v+1)(4v-1)w^{2}+(19-7v)v-20}{(1-v)^{4}}

(211)

\displaystyle+\frac{12v^{2}-45v+34}{(1-v)^{4}(1-v\,w)}+\frac{15v-23}{(1-v)^{3}(1-v\,w)^{2}}+\frac{6}{(1-v)^{2}(1-v\,w)^{3}}\,.

\displaystyle\hat{\sigma}_{x0}^{gg\to q^{\prime}}(v,w,\chi_{m},\chi_{\mu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle A_{1,x0}^{gg\to q^{\prime}}(v,\chi_{m},\chi_{\mu})\delta(1-w)+A_{2,x0}^{gg\to q^{\prime}}(v)\frac{1}{(1-w)_{+}}

(212)

\displaystyle+A_{3,x0}^{gg\to q^{\prime}}(v,w)\ln(\chi_{m})+A_{4,x0}^{gg\to q^{\prime}}(v,w)\ln(1-w)

\displaystyle+A_{5,x0}^{gg\to q^{\prime}}(v,w)\ln(\chi_{\mu})+A_{6,x0}^{gg\to q^{\prime}}(v,w)\ln(1-v\,w)

\displaystyle+A_{7,x0}^{gg\to q^{\prime}}(v,w)\ln(1-v)+A_{8,x0}^{gg\to q^{\prime}}(v,w)\ln(w)+A_{9,x0}^{gg\to q^{\prime}}(v,w)\,

with the following nine coefficient functions:

\displaystyle A_{1,x0}^{gg\to q^{\prime}}(v,\chi_{m},\chi_{\mu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{2(v-1)v+1}{(1-v)^{4}}\ln(\chi_{m})-\frac{1+v^{2}}{(1-v)^{4}}\ln(\chi_{\mu})+\frac{(2-v)^{2}}{(1-v)^{4}}+\frac{(2-3v)v-2}{(1-v)^{4}}\ln(1-v)\,,

\displaystyle A_{2,x0}^{gg\to q^{\prime}}(v)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{-2+2v-3v^{2}}{(1-v)^{4}}\,,

\displaystyle A_{3,x0}^{gg\to q^{\prime}}(v,w)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{v^{2}\left(4w^{2}-2\right)+4v-4}{(1-v)^{4}}+\frac{4(2-v)v^{2}w^{2}+(3-v)(1-3vw)}{(1-v)^{3}(1-v\,w)^{3}}\,,

\displaystyle A_{4}^{gg\to q^{\prime}}(v,w)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{v\left(v\left(6w^{2}-3\right)+4\right)+2w^{2}-5}{(1-v)^{4}}+\frac{4(2-v)v^{2}w^{2}+(3-v)(1-3vw)}{(1-v)^{3}(1-v\,w)^{3}}\,,

\displaystyle A_{5,x0}^{gg\to q^{\prime}}(v,w)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{\left(1+v^{2}\right)\left(1-2w^{2}\right)}{(1-v)^{4}}\,,

\displaystyle A_{6}^{gg\to q^{\prime}}(v,w)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{2v^{2}w^{2}(-4v^{2}w^{2}(3-vw)+v(v+9)w+v-3)}{(1-v)^{3}(1-v\,w)^{3}}\,,

\displaystyle A_{7,x0}^{gg\to q^{\prime}}(v,w)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\left((8v-2)v^{2}+2\right)w^{2}-v^{2}-4v+1}{(1-v)^{4}}-\frac{4(2-v)v^{2}w^{2}+(3-v)(1-3vw)}{(1-v)^{3}(1-v\,w)^{3}}\,,

\displaystyle A_{8,x0}^{gg\to q^{\prime}}(v,w)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{2\left(4v^{2}+v+1\right)w^{2}+v-3}{(1-v)^{3}}-\frac{4(2-v)v^{2}w^{2}+(3-v)(1-3vw)}{(1-v)^{3}(1-v\,w)^{3}}\,,

\displaystyle A_{9,x0}^{gg\to q^{\prime}}(v,w)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2\left(7v^{2}+v-4\right)w^{2}+8v^{2}+8(1-2v)vw-23v+25}{(1-v)^{4}}

(213)

\displaystyle-\frac{12v^{2}-49v+38}{(1-v)^{4}(1-v\,w)}-\frac{15v-23}{(1-v)^{3}(1-v\,w)^{2}}-\frac{6}{(1-v)^{2}(1-v\,w)^{3}}\,.

D.6
Specific Contributions for Jet Production

In this last subsection, we present the jet-specific partonic factors that appear in (
82
). The variable
v
in the following is to be understood as
v=v_{1}=1+\tfrac{t}{s}
. For the SGP term, we divide the partonic factors into two parts,

\hat{\sigma}_{\mathrm{SGP,\,jet},F}^{qg\to\mathrm{jet}(q+g)}(w,R,\mu)=C_{F}\,\hat{\sigma}_{\mathrm{SGP,\,jet},F}^{qg\to\mathrm{jet}(q)}(w,R,\mu)+C_{F}\,\hat{\sigma}_{\mathrm{SGP,\,jet},F}^{qg\to\mathrm{jet}(g)}(w,R,\mu)\,,

(214)

where the part corresponding to former quark fragmentation is given as

\displaystyle\hat{\sigma}_{\mathrm{SGP,\,jet},F}^{qg\to\mathrm{jet}(q)}(w,R,\mu)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle A_{0,\mathrm{SGP,\,jet},F}^{qg\to\mathrm{jet}(q)}(R,\mu)\,\delta(1-w)+A_{1,\mathrm{SGP,\,jet},F}^{qg\to\mathrm{jet}(q)}\,\left(\frac{\ln(1-w)}{1-w}\right)_{+}

(215)

\displaystyle+A_{2,\mathrm{SGP,\,jet},F}^{qg\to\mathrm{jet}(q)}(R,\mu)\,\frac{1}{(1-w)_{+}}

\displaystyle+A_{3,\mathrm{SGP,\,jet},F}^{qg\to\mathrm{jet}(q)}\,\ln\left(R^{2}v^{2}(1-w)^{2}\,\tfrac{t\,u}{s\,\mu^{2}}\right)+A_{4,\mathrm{SGP,\,jet},F}^{qg\to\mathrm{jet}(q)},

with

\displaystyle A_{0,\mathrm{SGP,\,jet},F}^{qg\to\mathrm{jet}(q)}(R,\mu)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{4v^{3}+9v^{2}+12v+1-4(1+v^{2})\,\ln(v)}{2(1-v)^{3}}\ln\left(R^{2}\,\tfrac{t\,u}{s\,\mu^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle-2\frac{1+v^{2}}{(1-v)^{3}}\ln^{2}(v)+4\frac{(1+v)^{3}}{(1-v)^{3}}\ln(v)+\frac{1+v^{2}}{(1-v)^{3}}\left(\tfrac{13}{2}-\tfrac{2}{3}\pi^{2}\right)\,,

\displaystyle A_{1,\mathrm{SGP,\,jet},F}^{qg\to\mathrm{jet}(q)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-4\frac{1+v^{2}}{(1-v)^{3}}\,,

\displaystyle A_{2,\mathrm{SGP,\,jet},F}^{qg\to\mathrm{jet}(q)}(R,\mu)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 4\frac{(1+v)^{3}-(1+v^{2})\ln(v)}{(1-v)^{3}}-2\frac{1+v^{2}}{(1-v)^{3}}\,\ln\left(R^{2}\tfrac{t\,u}{s\,\mu^{2}}\right)\,,

\displaystyle A_{3,\mathrm{SGP,\,jet},F}^{qg\to\mathrm{jet}(q)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{6v^{4}w^{4}}{(1-v)^{3}}+\frac{4v^{3}(2v-3)w^{3}}{(1-v)^{3}}-\frac{v^{2}\left(3v^{2}-8v+9\right)w^{2}}{(1-v)^{3}}+2\frac{1+v^{2}}{(1-v)^{3}}\,,

\displaystyle A_{4,\mathrm{SGP,\,jet},F}^{qg\to\mathrm{jet}(q)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{10v^{4}w^{4}}{(1-v)^{3}}+4\frac{v^{3}(3v-4)w^{3}}{(1-v)^{3}}-\frac{v^{2}\left(5v^{2}-8v+19\right)w^{2}}{(1-v)^{3}}

(216)

\displaystyle-\frac{4v\left(v^{2}+2v+3\right)w}{(1-v)^{3}}-4\frac{(1+v)^{3}}{(1-v)^{3}}\,.

We note that the explicit form of the coefficient
A_{0,\mathrm{SGP,\,jet},F}^{qg\to\mathrm{jet}(q)}
depends on the jet algorithm adopted
[
86
]
, and the
one given here applies to the anti-
k_{T}
algorithm.

The specific form of the contribution from former gluon fragmentation reads,

\displaystyle\hat{\sigma}_{\mathrm{SGP,\,jet},F}^{qg\to\mathrm{jet}(g)}(w,R,\mu)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle A_{0,\mathrm{SGP,\,jet},F}^{qg\to\mathrm{jet}(g)}(R,\mu)\,\delta(1-w)+A_{1,\mathrm{SGP,\,jet},F}^{qg\to\mathrm{jet}(g)}\,\frac{1}{(1-w)_{+}}

(217)

\displaystyle+A_{2,\mathrm{SGP,\,jet},F}^{qg\to\mathrm{jet}(g)}\,\ln\left(R^{2}\,v^{2}(1-w)^{2}\,\tfrac{t\,u}{s\,\mu^{2}}\right)+A_{3,\mathrm{SGP,\,jet},F}^{qg\to\mathrm{jet}(g)},

with

\displaystyle A_{0,\mathrm{SGP,\,jet},F}^{qg\to\mathrm{jet}(g)}(R,\mu)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{v\left(1+v^{2}\right)}{(1-v)^{3}}\left(\ln\left(R^{2}\tfrac{t\,u}{s\,\mu^{2}}\right)+2\ln(v)+1\right)\,,

\displaystyle A_{1,\mathrm{SGP,\,jet},F}^{qg\to\mathrm{jet}(g)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{2v\left(1+v^{2}\right)}{(1-v)^{3}}\,,

\displaystyle A_{2,\mathrm{SGP,\,jet},F}^{qg\to\mathrm{jet}(g)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{6v^{4}w^{4}}{(1-v)^{3}}-\frac{8v^{4}w^{3}}{(1-v)^{3}}+\frac{\left(3v^{2}-2v+3\right)v^{2}w^{2}}{(1-v)^{3}}+\frac{4}{1-v+v\,w}-\frac{2(1-v)}{(1-v+v\,w)^{2}}-\frac{2}{1-v}\,,

\displaystyle A_{3,\mathrm{SGP,\,jet},F}^{qg\to\mathrm{jet}(g)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{10v^{4}w^{4}}{(1-v)^{3}}-\frac{4v^{3}(3v-2)w^{3}}{(1-v)^{3}}+\frac{v^{2}\left(5v^{2}-10v+9\right)w^{2}}{(1-v)^{3}}-\frac{2v\left(v^{2}-4v+1\right)w}{(1-v)^{3}}

(218)

\displaystyle-\frac{2\left(v^{3}-6v^{2}+5v-2\right)}{(1-v)^{3}}-\frac{4(1-v)}{1-v+v\,w}\,.

The coefficient for the derivative term is simpler, contains only distributions, and reads,

\displaystyle\hskip-19.91684pt\hat{\sigma}_{\mathrm{SGP,\,jet},F^{\prime}}^{qg\to\mathrm{jet}(q)}(w,R,\mu)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C_{F}\left(A_{0,\mathrm{SGP,\,jet},F^{\prime}}^{qg\to\mathrm{jet}(q)}(R,\mu)\delta(1-w)+A_{1,\mathrm{SGP,\,jet},F^{\prime}}^{qg\to\mathrm{jet}(q)}\left(\frac{\ln(1-w)}{1-w}\right)_{+}\right.

(219)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\left.+A_{2,\mathrm{SGP,\,jet},F^{\prime}}^{qg\to\mathrm{jet}(q)}\frac{1}{(1-w)_{+}}\right)\,,

with

\displaystyle A_{0,\mathrm{SGP,\,jet},F^{\prime}}^{qg\to\mathrm{jet}(q)}(R,\mu)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1+v^{2}}{(1-v)^{3}}\left[2\ln^{2}(v)+(\tfrac{3}{2}+2\,\ln(v))\ln\left(R^{2}\,\tfrac{t\,u}{s\,\mu^{2}}\right)-\tfrac{13}{2}+\tfrac{2}{3}\pi^{2}\right]\,,

\displaystyle A_{1,\mathrm{SGP,\,jet},F^{\prime}}^{qg\to\mathrm{jet}(q)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{4\left(1+v^{2}\right)}{(1-v)^{3}}\,,

\displaystyle A_{2,\mathrm{SGP,\,jet},F^{\prime}}^{qg\to\mathrm{jet}(q)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{2\left(1+v^{2}\right)}{(1-v)^{3}}\ln\left(R^{2}\,\tfrac{t\,u}{s\,\mu^{2}}\right)-\frac{4\left(1+v^{2}\right)}{(1-v)^{3}}\ln(v)\,.

(220)

Again,
A_{0,\mathrm{SGP,\,jet},F^{\prime}}^{qg\to\mathrm{jet}(q)}
is specific to the anti-
k_{T}
algorithm.

Appendix E
Model Input for the Quark-Gluon-Quark Correlation Functions
F
and
G

In this appendix, we provide details on the model input for the quark-gluon-quark correlation functions
F(x,x^{\prime})
and
G(x,x^{\prime})
that we use for our numerical studies discussed in Section
IV
.
We start with a discussion of the soft-gluon pole matrix element
F^{q}(x,x)
. It can be related to the first TMD moment of the Sivers function using (
10
), which in turn has been extracted in the literature from global data fits, in particular from SIDIS. For more information on the status of the fits, we refer the reader to the recent comprehensive review of TMDs
[
100
]
. For our work, we rely on one of the earliest extractions of the first moment of the Sivers function, given in
[
101
]
, the reason being its simple numerical implementation. We stress once more that the purpose of our numerical study is to explore the impact of NLO effects on the single-spin observable
A_{RL}
in (
88
) rather than to provide fully phenomenologically relevant predictions. For this purpose, the early parameterization of
[
101
]
suffices.

In Ref.
[
101
]
, the first TMD moment of the Sivers function is parameterized at a scale
\mu_{0}=1.55\,\mathrm{GeV}
as follows:

\pi\,F^{q}(x,x,\mu_{0})=f_{1T}^{\perp(1),q}(x,\mu_{0})=-\tfrac{1}{2}N^{q}(x)\,f_{1}^{q}(x,\mu_{0})\,\sqrt{2\mathrm{e}}\,\frac{M_{1}^{3}\,\langle k_{T}^{2}\rangle}{M\,(M_{1}^{2}+\langle k_{T}^{2}\rangle)^{2}}\,,

(221)

with
M
the nucleon mass, the flavor-independent mass parameters
M_{1}=0.583\,\mathrm{GeV}
and
\langle k_{T}^{2}\rangle=0.25\,\mathrm{GeV}^{2}
,
\mathrm{e}=2.7182\ldots
the Euler constant, and
f_{1}^{q}(x,\mu_{0})
the MSTW2008 quark PDF for flavor
q

[
90
]
evaluated at the scale
\mu_{0}
. The flavor-dependent factor
N^{q}(x)
has the form

N^{q}(x)=N^{q}\,x^{\alpha_{q}}(1-x)^{\beta_{q}}\,\frac{(\alpha_{q}+\beta_{q})^{\alpha_{q}+\beta_{q}}}{\alpha_{q}^{\alpha_{q}}\,\beta_{q}^{\beta_{q}}}\,,

with flavor-dependent parameters
N^{q}
,
\alpha_{q}
,
\beta_{q}
whose values can be found in Ref.
[
101
]
. The flavors included in (
221
) are
q=u,d,s
. The SGP matrix element for negative
x
can be related via charge conjugation (see the discussion below (
93
)) to the first TMD moment of the antiquark Sivers function (
\bar{q}=\bar{u},\bar{d},\bar{s}
),

\pi\,F^{q}(-x,-x,\mu_{0})=f_{1T}^{\perp(1),\bar{q}}(x,\mu_{0})\,.

(222)

As can be seen from Eq. (
5
), the SGP input (
221
) is already sufficient to produce predictions for the right-left asymmetry
A_{RL}
at LO.
However, at NLO accuracy we need input for
F
and
G
on their full support. Both functions are essentially unknown on the “off-diagonal” support
x\neq x^{\prime}
and, to the best of our knowledge, have never been extracted from data. In order to study the NLO effects, we therefore have to resort to models for these correlation functions.

As a first step, we rewrite a specific point
(x,x^{\prime})
within the support of the functions
F^{q}
and
G^{q}
in terms of “polar coordinates”, i.e.
x(r,\varphi)=r\,\cos(\varphi+\tfrac{\pi}{4})
,
x^{\prime}(r,\varphi)=r\,\sin(\varphi+\tfrac{\pi}{4})
, with

\displaystyle r

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{x^{2}+(x^{\prime})^{2}}\,,

\displaystyle\varphi

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} -\tfrac{\pi}{4}&+\arctan(x^{\prime}/x),\quad x\geq 0\,,x^{\prime}\geq x\,,\\
\tfrac{3\pi}{4}&+\arctan(x^{\prime}/x),\quad x<0\,,\\
\tfrac{7\pi}{4}&+\arctan(x^{\prime}/x),\quad x\geq 0\,,x^{\prime}<x\,.\end{aligned}\right.

(223)

Note that we count the polar angle
\varphi
from the “diagonal” axis of support (
x^{\prime}=x
) instead of from the
x
-axis. We then consider the
qgq
correlation functions as functions of
r
and
\varphi
, with
r\in\,]0,\sqrt{2}]
,
\varphi\in[0,2\pi]
. The next observation is that both
F(r,\varphi)
and
G(r,\varphi)
are
2\pi
-periodic in
\varphi
, that is,
F(r,\varphi)=F(r,\varphi+2\pi)
and
G(r,\varphi)=G(r,\varphi+2\pi)
. This feature allows us to express both functions as Fourier series,

\displaystyle F^{q}(r,\varphi)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\,\left[A^{q}_{n}(r)\,\cos(n\varphi)+D^{q}_{n}(r)\,\sin(n\varphi)\right]\,,

\displaystyle G^{q}(r,\varphi)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\,\left[C^{q}_{n}(r)\,\cos(n\varphi)+B^{q}_{n}(r)\,\sin(n\varphi)\right]\,.

(224)

The important symmetry constraints (
94
) of the correlation functions under
x\leftrightarrow x^{\prime}
can be conveniently implemented into the Fourier Series (
224
). In particular, it is easy to see that the symmetry of
F
, i.e.
F(x,x^{\prime})=+F(x^{\prime},x)\Leftrightarrow F(r,\varphi)=+F(r,2\pi-\varphi)
, enforces that all Fourier coefficients
D_{n}(r)
vanish. Similarly, antisymmetry of
G
, that is,
G(x,x^{\prime})=-G(x^{\prime},x)\Leftrightarrow G(r,\varphi)=-G(r,2\pi-\varphi)
, means that all Fourier coefficients
C_{n}(r)
also vanish.

Furthermore, we can easily implement the constraint (
10
) as follows,

\displaystyle F^{q}(r,\varphi=0)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{\pi}\,f_{1T}^{\perp(1),q}(\tfrac{r}{\sqrt{2}})=A^{q}_{0}(r)+A^{q}_{1}(r)+A^{q}_{2}(r)+A^{q}_{3}(r)+...\,,

\displaystyle F^{q}(r,\varphi=\pi)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{\pi}\,f_{1T}^{\perp(1),\bar{q}}(\tfrac{r}{\sqrt{2}})=A^{q}_{0}(r)-A^{q}_{1}(r)+A^{q}_{2}(r)-A^{q}_{3}(r)+...\,.

We can solve these constraints for the Fourier coefficients for
A_{0}
and
A_{1}
, and obtain,

\displaystyle A_{0}^{q}(r)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2\pi}\left(f_{1T}^{\perp(1),q}(\tfrac{r}{\sqrt{2}})+f_{1T}^{\perp(1),\bar{q}}(\tfrac{r}{\sqrt{2}})\right)-A_{2}^{q}(r)-A_{4}^{q}(r)-A_{6}^{q}(r)-\ldots\,,

\displaystyle A_{1}^{q}(r)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2\pi}\left(f_{1T}^{\perp(1),q}(\tfrac{r}{\sqrt{2}})-f_{1T}^{\perp(1),\bar{q}}(\tfrac{r}{\sqrt{2}})\right)-A_{3}^{q}(r)-A_{5}^{q}(r)-A_{7}^{q}(r)-\ldots\,.

(225)

Implementing (
225
) into (
224
) yields, (
q\pm\bar{q}
denotes the sum/difference of quark and antiquark distributions as in (
225
))

\displaystyle F^{q}(r,\varphi)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2\pi}f_{1T}^{\perp(1),q+\bar{q}}(\tfrac{r}{\sqrt{2}})+\tfrac{1}{2\pi}f_{1T}^{\perp(1),q-\bar{q}}(\tfrac{r}{\sqrt{2}})\,\cos(\varphi)

\displaystyle\hskip-56.9055pt+\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\left[A_{2n}^{q}(r)\,(\cos(2n\varphi)-1)\right]+\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\left[A_{2n+1}^{q}(r)\,(\cos((2n+1)\varphi)-\cos(\varphi))\right]\,,

\displaystyle G^{q}(r,\varphi)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\,\left[B^{q}_{n}(r)\,\sin(n\varphi)\right]\,.

(226)

Up to this point, the two Fourier expansions in (
226
) are exact and model-independent. In particular, the Fourier coefficients
A_{n}(r)
,
B_{n}(r)
depend on
r=\sqrt{x^{2}+(x^{\prime})^{2}}
. In other words, for every Fourier component
n
there are two (unknown) functions
A_{n}(r)
,
B_{n}(r)
that should ideally be fitted to experimental data. This is of course an impossible task to do for all Fourier components. However, the series (
226
) are quite useful for building a parameterization that can be used as input for the numerical study of the observable
A_{RL}
in (
88
) at NLO. For this, we need to apply some simplifying assumptions.

First, we note that often Fourier series converges reasonably fast on an interval
[0,2\pi]
. Whether it does, depends of course on the series, but let us assume that we achieve a reasonable approximation to the “true” correlation functions already with the first six Fourier components for each flavor. To be specific, we assume that all Fourier coefficients for
n\geq 8
in case of
F
and for
n\geq 7
in case of
G
vanish, i.e.,
A^{q}_{n\geq 8}(r)=0
,
B^{q}_{n\geq 7}(r)=0
. If this assumption turns out to be wrong for some reason, for instance, because the explanation of experimental data may require a higher precision, one is free to add more Fourier components and truncate the series (
226
) at higher
n
.

Secondly, we may make further assumptions about the functional form of the Fourier coefficients
A^{q}_{n}(r)
,
B^{q}_{n}(r)
. In a first step, we may introduce modified coefficients
a_{n}^{q}(r)
,
b_{n}^{q}(r)
according to

\displaystyle A^{q}_{n=2,4,6,...}(r)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2\pi}f_{1T}^{\perp(1),q+\bar{q}}(\tfrac{r}{\sqrt{2}})\,a^{q}_{n=2,4,6,...}(r)\,,

\displaystyle A^{q}_{n=1,3,5,...}(r)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2\pi}f_{1T}^{\perp(1),q-\bar{q}}(\tfrac{r}{\sqrt{2}})\,a^{q}_{n=1,3,5,...}(r)\,,

\displaystyle B^{q}_{n}(r)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle-\tfrac{1}{\pi}f_{1T}^{\perp(1),q+\bar{q}}(\tfrac{r}{\sqrt{2}})\,b^{q}_{n}(r)\,.

(227)

The idea of this modification is the assumption that the “size” or “scale” of the quark-gluon-quark correlation functions
F
and
G
is roughly set by the SGP diagonal determined by the extraction (
221
). Under this assumption, the modified coefficients
a_{n}(r)
,
b_{n}(r)
may vary on the order of magnitude of 1, but not, say, 1000.

Third, we may even go one step further and approximate the modified coefficients in (
227
) to be effectively constants. We replace the functions
a_{n}^{q}(r)
,
b_{n}^{q}(r)
by their mean values
a_{n}^{q}(r)\to\langle a_{n}^{q}(r)\rangle=a_{n}^{q}
,
b_{n}^{q}(r)\to\langle b_{n}^{q}(r)\rangle=b_{n}^{q}
.

Applying these assumptions, we end up with the following model ansÃ¤tze for the quark-gluon-quark correlation functions:

\displaystyle F^{q}(r,\varphi)\Big|_{\mathrm{model}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2\pi}f_{1T}^{\perp(1),q+\bar{q}}(\tfrac{r}{\sqrt{2}})\,\left[1+\sum_{n=1}^{3}\left[a_{2n}^{q}\,(\cos(2n\varphi)-1)\right]\right]

\displaystyle\hskip-56.9055pt+\tfrac{1}{2\pi}f_{1T}^{\perp(1),q-\bar{q}}(\tfrac{r}{\sqrt{2}})\,\left[\cos(\varphi)+\sum_{n=1}^{3}\left[a_{2n+1}^{q}\,(\cos((2n+1)\varphi)-\cos(\varphi))\right]\right]\,,

\displaystyle G^{q}(r,\varphi)\Big|_{\mathrm{model}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\tfrac{1}{\pi}f_{1T}^{\perp(1),q+\bar{q}}(\tfrac{r}{\sqrt{2}})\,\sum_{n=1}^{6}\,\left[b^{q}_{n}\,\sin(n\varphi)\right]\,.

(228)

Effectively, the models (
228
) allow us to describe each of the functions
F
,
G
by six parameters, separately for each flavor.
We may collect these parameters as entries in a vector in the following way (note the specific ordering of the even and odd Fourier coefficients for
F
):

\displaystyle\bm{a}^{q}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(a_{2}^{q},a_{4}^{q},a_{6}^{q};a_{3}^{q},a_{5}^{q},a_{7}^{q}\right)\,,

\displaystyle\bm{b}^{q}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(b_{1}^{q},b_{2}^{q},b_{3}^{q},b_{4}^{q},b_{5}^{q},b_{6}^{q}\right)\,.

(229)

Note that no matter the values the vectors
\bm{a}^{q}
,
\bm{b}^{q}
take, the soft-gluon pole is always provided by the extraction (
221
). It is the only constraint available from data for the functions
F
and
G
.

In order to smoothen the transition of the functions
F
and
G
at the boundaries
|x|=1
,
|x^{\prime}|=1
,
|x-x^{\prime}|=1
of their support it is helpful to introduce an envelope function, for example of the following form:

\displaystyle e(x,x^{\prime})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\frac{2}{1+\mathrm{e}^{50\,(x^{2}-1)^{3}}}-1\right)\,\left(\frac{2}{1+\mathrm{e}^{50\,((x^{\prime})^{2}-1)^{3}}}-1\right)\times

(230)

\displaystyle\left(\frac{2}{1+\mathrm{e}^{50\,((x-x^{\prime})^{2}-1)^{3}}}-1\right)\theta(1-|x|)\theta(1-|x^{\prime}|)\theta(1-|x-x^{\prime}|)\,.

The function
e(x,x^{\prime})
in (
230
) is approximately unity, except in the vicinity of the boundary
|x|=1
,
|x^{\prime}|=1
,
|x-x^{\prime}|=1
. Multiplying the function
e(x,x^{\prime})
in (
230
) with the model expressions (
229
) does not significantly alter the model but ensures smoothness of the functions
F
and
G
even at the boundary. In particular, it ensures
F(x,1)=F(1,x^{\prime})=G(x,1)=G(1,x^{\prime})=0
and
(\partial_{2}F)(x,1)=(\partial_{1}F)(1,x^{\prime})=(\partial_{2}G)(x,1)=(\partial_{1}G)(1,x^{\prime})=0
.
Therefore, in the following, we perform the replacement

F^{q}(r,\varphi)\Big|_{\mathrm{model}}\to F^{q}(r,\varphi)\Big|_{\mathrm{model}}e(x,x^{\prime})\quad;\quad G^{q}(r,\varphi)\Big|_{\mathrm{model}}\to G^{q}(r,\varphi)\Big|_{\mathrm{model}}e(x,x^{\prime}).

Constraints from Lattice QCD

There exists another source of (somewhat indirect) information on
F
from lattice QCD that one may apply here as well. It turns out that one can express the second moment of the genuine twist-3 part of the DIS structure function
\bar{g}_{2}
, the so-called
d_{2}
moment, in terms of the fully integrated quark-gluon-quark function
F
. This feature has been discussed, for example, in
[
102
,
103
]
. Interestingly, one can interpret the moment
d_{2}
as a probe of the color Lorentz force, mediated by the strong force in the nucleon
[
104
,
105
]
.
The connection between
d_{2}
and
F
is as follows,

d_{2}^{q}=-\int_{-1}^{1}\mathrm{d}x\int_{x-1}^{1}\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}\,F^{q}(x,x^{\prime})\,.

(231)

In Refs.
[
106
,
107
,
108
,
109
]
the
d_{2}
moments for up and down quarks were computed on the lattice.
The values found in
[
108
]
were reported as

\displaystyle d_{2}^{u}=-0.00365(25)\;;

\displaystyle d_{2}^{d}=0\,.

(232)

Despite the limitations related to unphysical pion masses or renormalization schemes, these values give us another hint at the size of the function
F
.
Due to the linearity of the model (
228
) in the Fourier coefficients
a^{q}
, it is easy to implement the lattice constraint (
232
). We simply insert Eq. (
228
) into (
231
) and obtain

A_{0}^{q}+A_{2}^{q}\,a_{2}^{q}+A_{4}^{q}\,a_{4}^{q}+A_{6}^{q}\,a_{6}^{q}+A_{3}^{q}\,a_{3}^{q}+A_{5}^{q}\,a_{5}^{q}+A_{7}^{q}\,a_{7}^{q}=d_{2}^{q}\,,

(233)

where

\displaystyle A_{0}^{q}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle-\int_{-1}^{1}\mathrm{d}x\int_{-1}^{1}\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}\,F^{q}(r,\varphi)\Big|_{\mathrm{model},\,\bm{a}^{q}=\bm{0}}\,,

\displaystyle A_{i}^{q}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle-A_{0}^{q}-\int_{-1}^{1}\mathrm{d}x\int_{-1}^{1}\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}\,F^{q}(r,\varphi)\Big|_{\mathrm{model},\,a_{i}^{q}=1,\,a^{q}_{j\neq i}=0}\,.

(234)

By solving the constraints (
233
) for the Fourier coefficient
a_{2}^{q}
(for example) we can express it as a function of the remaining coefficients and thus remove one degree of freedom,

a_{2}^{q}=(d_{2}^{q}-(A_{0}^{q}+A_{4}^{q}\,a_{4}^{q}+A_{6}^{q}\,a_{6}^{q}+A_{3}^{q}\,a_{3}^{q}+A_{5}^{q}\,a_{5}^{q}+A_{7}^{q}\,a_{7}^{q}))/A_{2}^{q}\,.

(235)

We emphasize that no constraint similar to (
233
) is known for the correlation function
G
.

In the following we will consider three scenarios for the correlation functions based on three specific choices of Fourier coefficients (
229
). Each of the following scenarios is consistent with the constraint (
231
) for the
d_{2}^{u},d_{2}^{d}
.
We note that all our scenarios are formulated
in terms of the first TMD moments of the Sivers functions
f_{1T}^{\perp(1),q+\bar{q}}
and hence, via Eq. (
221
),
in terms of the unpolarized twist-2 PDFs
f_{1}^{q}
. The corresponding relations can strictly hold only at one scale
\mu_{0}
,
since the evolutions of the
f_{1T}^{\perp(1),q+\bar{q}}
and
f_{1}^{q}
differ. Nevertheless, as discussed in Sec.
IV
,
in our numerical studies we assume
f_{1T}^{\perp(1),q+\bar{q}}
and
f_{1}^{q}
to be related in the same way at all scales,
simply as a rough means of mimicking the scale evolution of the twist-3 quark correlation functions.

Figure 22:
Correlation functions
F^{u}(x,x^{\prime})

(left)
and
F^{d}(x,x^{\prime})

(right)
for Scenario 0.

Figure 23:
Same as Fig.
22
, but for Scenario 1.

E.1
Scenario 0

For the first scenario we choose the Fourier coefficients (
229
) as follows:

\displaystyle\bm{a}^{u}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(1.1578,0,0;0,0,0\right)\,,

\displaystyle\bm{a}^{d}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(1.0173,0,0;0,0,0\right)\,,

\displaystyle\bm{a}^{s}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(0,0,0;0,0,0\right)\,,

\displaystyle\bm{b}^{u}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(0,0,0,0,0,0\right)\,,

\displaystyle\bm{b}^{d}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(0,0,0,0,0,0\right)\,,

\displaystyle\bm{b}^{s}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(0,0,0,0,0,0\right)\,.

(236)

This choice sets the correlation function
G
basically to zero, and provides a correlation function
F
that is as “levelled” as possible by utilizing only the first three Fourier components
\cos(0\varphi)
,
\cos(1\varphi)
,
\cos(2\varphi)
in the model ansatz (
228
). Note that an additional choice
a_{2}^{q}=0
would violate the
d_{2}
-constraint (
233
). We show the resulting
F^{q}
as contour plots in Fig.
22
. We expect that NLO contributions to the asymmetry
A_{RL}
within this scenario are minimal, since
G=0
and the plots in Fig.
22
show little “structure” of
F^{q}
.

E.2
Scenario 1

In this scenario we populate most of the Fourier coefficients (
229
) for the
u
- and
d
-quark correlation functions with relatively moderate values that vary between
-1
and
1
. Doing so, we also generate a nonvanishing correlation function
G
. As before we choose the values of the Fourier coefficients such that the
d_{2}
-constraint from Lattice QCD (
234
) is satisfied. We consider this scenario somewhat more “realistic” as far as the sizes of the correlation functions are concerned.
To be specific, we choose the Fourier coefficients in Scenario 1 as

\displaystyle\bm{a}^{u}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(2.5308,-\tfrac{2}{3},-\tfrac{2}{3};-\tfrac{1}{3},-1,-\tfrac{1}{3}\right)\,,

\displaystyle\bm{a}^{d}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(-0.3429,\tfrac{2}{3},\tfrac{2}{3};\tfrac{1}{3},1,\tfrac{1}{3}\right)\,,

\displaystyle\bm{a}^{s}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(0,0,0;0,0,0\right)\,,

\displaystyle\bm{b}^{u}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(-2.5308,\tfrac{1}{3},\tfrac{2}{3},1,\tfrac{2}{3},\tfrac{1}{3}\right)\,,

\displaystyle\bm{b}^{d}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(0.3429,-\tfrac{1}{3},-\tfrac{2}{3},-1,-\tfrac{2}{3},-\tfrac{1}{3}\right)\,,

\displaystyle\bm{b}^{s}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(0,0,0,0,0,0\right)\,.

(237)

The contour plots for the resulting
u
- and
d
-quark correlation functions
F^{q}(x,x^{\prime})
are shown in Fig.
23
.
Figure
24
shows the corresponding correlation functions
G^{q}
for up and down quarks. Note that the symmetry and antisymmetry properties of
F
and
G
under exchange
x\leftrightarrow x^{\prime}
are well visible in Figs.
23
and
24
.

Figure 24:
Correlation functions
G^{u}(x,x^{\prime})

(left)
and
G^{d}(x,x^{\prime})

(right)
for Scenario 1.

E.3
Scenario 2

Figure 25:
Same as Fig.
22
, but for Scenario 2.

Figure 26:
Same as Fig.

, but for Scenario 2.

Finally, Scenario 2 is similar to Scenario 1, but with Fourier coefficients inflated by a factor of three in order to magnify the effects of the NLO corrections on

A_{RL}
. Specifically, we choose

\displaystyle\bm{a}^{u}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(5.2767,-2,-2;-1,-3,-1\right)\,,

\displaystyle\bm{a}^{d}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(-3.0634,2,2;1,3,1\right)\,,

\displaystyle\bm{a}^{s}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(0,0,0;0,0,0\right)\,,

\displaystyle\bm{b}^{u}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(-5.2767,1,2,3,2,1\right)\,,

\displaystyle\bm{b}^{d}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(3.0634,-1,-2,-3,-2,-1\right)\,,

\displaystyle\bm{b}^{s}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(0,0,0,0,0,0\right)\,.

(238)

As for the other two scenarios, the constraint (
231
) on
d_{2}
by lattice QCD is satisfied also here. The resulting contour plots for the
u
- and
d
-quark correlation functions
F^{q}
are shown in Fig.
25
, while Figure
26
presents the corresponding contour plots for
G
.
